@@ -182,6 +182,12 @@ Finding numbers refer to `docs/agent-runs/phase-01/review-report.md`. The human
 - **Test-stop hook remainder (finding 14).** Risk: `verify-tests-ran.sh` finds changed sources through `git status`, so Scala or sbt sources that Git ignores are invisible to it, and a `git status` error is silently treated as no changes. Mitigation: the definition of done still requires an observed `sbt check` pass in the owning agent's report, and the code-reviewer checks that test output.
 - **Git global option before the subcommand (re-review N2).** Risk: when a git global option that takes a separate value comes before the subcommand, `guard-dangerous.sh` misidentifies the subcommand, so the short `-o` output-file checks for `format-patch` and `archive` do not apply. Mitigation: the long `--output` and `--output-directory` forms are still caught, and no `allow` rule auto-approves the command, so it reaches the permission prompt.
 - **Unrecognized protected-path writes.** Risk: Bash writes to protected paths in forms the guard does not recognize, such as `rm -rf spec` or `touch .claude/x`, are not blocked by `guard-dangerous.sh` (see Hook limitations above). Mitigation: no `allow` rule auto-approves them, so they reach the permission prompt, where the human denies them; file-tool writes to the same paths are still blocked by `guard-paths.sh`.
+- **Guard false positive: a write verb in a command that names `labels/` (Phase 17 work order 3).** Risk: `guard-dangerous.sh` (not `guard-paths.sh`, which never sees Bash) blocks a read-only Bash command with `[protected-path-write]` when the command names `labels/` anywhere, even as a path that is only read, and one of its segments looks like a write. The guard removes quotes and splits the command at `;`, `&`, `|`, `(`, `)`, `{`, `}`, backticks, and newlines, including inside quoted and heredoc text. A segment that begins with a write verb (for example `patch`, `install`, `touch`, `ed`, or `cp`) is treated as a command. A wrapper segment (for example `find`, `xargs`, `env`, `python`, or `awk`) is blocked when it contains a write verb anywhere, as in `find labels/ -type f -exec grep -l patch {} +`. The same applies to every other protected path the guard recognizes. Mitigation: the false positive fails closed, so nothing runs and nothing is written; agents read `labels/` only in evaluation code paths (Human-owned artifacts above), so few agent commands name it; and the block message names the hook, the category, and the segment, and tells the agent to stop and report `BLOCKED-ON` instead of rewording the command, and the orchestrator routes that `BLOCKED-ON` result (Topology above).
+- **Guard false positive: a jq read combined with `tee` (Phase 17 work order 3).** Risk: `guard-dangerous.sh` blocks with `[protected-path-write]` a jq command that reads a protected file and pipes its output to `tee`, for example `jq '.permissions' .claude/settings.json | tee /tmp/x.json`. The guard checks whether the command names a protected path anywhere, not whether the write target is protected, and `tee` begins its own segment, so a copy into a temporary directory is blocked like a write to the protected file. The same holds for any reader combined with a write verb in command position. Mitigation: the false positive fails closed; the same write verb aimed at a temporary path passes when the command names no protected path; and the block message tells the agent to stop and report `BLOCKED-ON` instead of rewording the command.
+- **Guard false positive: `jq` combined with `env` (Phase 17 work order 3).** Risk: `guard-dangerous.sh` blocks any command that runs jq and contains the word `env` anywhere, with the category `[secret-jq-env]`, not `[protected-path-write]`; jq field access that names `.env` is blocked as `[secret-env-file]`. Both are described under Hook limitations above. Mitigation: the rule exists because jq's `env` built-in and `$ENV` print the whole process environment, which can hold secrets; the false positive fails closed; and the block message tells the agent to stop and report `BLOCKED-ON` instead of rewording the command.
+- **Guard false positive: `==>` in a grep pattern (Phase 17 work order 3).** Risk: `guard-dangerous.sh` removes quotes before it scans, so in `grep -n '==>' docs/agent-runs/phase-NN/agent-report.md` the `>` of the pattern is read as a redirection into `docs/agent-runs/`, and the read-only search is blocked with the category `[protected-path-redirect]`, not `[protected-path-write]`. Any `>` inside a quoted argument is blocked the same way when the next word (after optional spaces) contains `spec/`, `labels/`, `docs/agent-runs/`, `docs/agents.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.mcp.json`, `.claude/`, or `.git/`. Mitigation: the false positive fails closed, and the block message tells the agent to stop and report `BLOCKED-ON` instead of rewording the command.
+- **Client limit: Grep and Glob unavailable to subagents (Phase 17 work order 3).** Risk: in some sessions (Phase 15 sessions 9, 10, and 12) the Claude Code client did not provide the Grep and Glob tools to subagents, although both are in the tool fences above, so an agent could not search the repository for its inputs. Mitigation: work orders name every input by path, so no search is needed to find them. The missing tools remove capability and widen nothing.
+- **Client limit: the `aws-api` MCP server fails to connect (Phase 17 work order 3).** Risk: the `aws-api` server can fail to connect, so the agents granted it (`infra-engineer`, `deploy-engineer`, `experiment-engineer`, `incident-responder`) cannot make read-only AWS inspections through it. Mitigation: live AWS reads are human-run. A server that does not connect exposes no tools, so the failure removes access and widens nothing.

+# ScholarChain REST API reference
+
+Owner: documentation-engineer. Taken from the route code in [`modules/services/src/main/scala/scholarchain/services/`](../modules/services/src/main/scala/scholarchain/services/) (`Services.scala`, `Models.scala`, `Http.scala`), the error codes in [`modules/core/src/main/scala/scholarchain/core/Enums.scala`](../modules/core/src/main/scala/scholarchain/core/Enums.scala) (`ErrorCode`), and the examples under [`modules/services/openapi/examples/`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/). The machine-readable contract is [`modules/services/openapi.yaml`](../modules/services/openapi.yaml); `ServicesContractSpec` parses every example against the Scala models. Where this file and the code disagree, the code is right.
+
+The example files were produced by the fake-mode test fixtures (three fictional researchers), except the hand-written query-request examples (`openapi.yaml` info text). They are not results from the deployed corpus. Results from the deployed window 3 runtime are in [`operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z/`](../operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z/); that runtime was destroyed on Nov 20, 2026.
+
+## Services and routes
+
+| Service (port) | Method | Path | Key needed |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| query-api (8080) | POST | `/v1/query` | yes |
+| query-api | GET | `/v1/jobs/{jobId}` | yes |
+| query-api | GET | `/v1/jobs/{jobId}/result` | yes |
+| query-api | GET | `/v1/explain/{queryId}` | yes |
+| graph-evidence-api (8081) | GET | `/v1/evidence/{evidenceId}` | yes |
+| graph-evidence-api | GET | `/v1/graph/nodes/{nodeId}` | yes |
+| graph-evidence-api | GET | `/v1/graph/nodes/{nodeId}/neighbors` | yes |
+| every service, worker (8082) included | GET | `/healthz` | **no** (public) |
+| every service | GET | `/readyz` | **no** (public) |
+| every service | GET | `/metrics` | yes (present when `services.metrics-enabled` is true; not routed by the ALB) |
+
+Ports are `services.query-api.port`, `services.graph-evidence-api.port` and `services.worker.port` in [`scholarchain.conf`](../modules/core/src/main/resources/scholarchain.conf). Behind the ALB the paths above are routed by prefix (see [`infra/terraform/README.md`](../infra/terraform/README.md) "ECS and the ALB").
+
+## Conventions (all routes)
+
+- **Authentication.** Header `X-Api-Key`, compared in constant time with the value of the environment variable that `services.api-key-env` names (`SCHOLARCHAIN_API_KEY`, injected by ECS from Secrets Manager). Missing or wrong key: 401 `UNAUTHORIZED`. Only `/healthz` and `/readyz` are public. `FORBIDDEN` (403) is reserved and unused in the single-key deployment.
+- **Idempotency.** `POST /v1/query` accepts `Idempotency-Key` (1 to 255 visible ASCII characters; stored hashed; expires after `services.ttl.idempotency-hours`, 24). The same key and canonical body replays the stored response with header `Idempotent-Replay: true`. The same key with a different body is 409 `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`. Without a key every POST is a new query. After a 503 `OVERLOADED` on a sync POST, retry with a new key ([`modules/services/SERVICES.md`](../modules/services/SERVICES.md) section 3).
+- **Request and trace IDs.** `X-Request-Id` and `X-Trace-Id` are echoed when the client sends 1 to 128 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9._:-]`; otherwise the service generates `req-<32 hex>` and `trace-<32 hex>`. Both are on every response and in every error body.
+- **Content type.** A POST body must be `Content-Type: application/json`, at most `services.max-body-bytes` (65,536) bytes. Responses are `application/json; charset=utf-8` with `Cache-Control: no-store`.
+- **Query parameters.** Only the neighbors route takes query parameters; any other route answers 400 `INVALID_FIELD` when one is sent.
+- **Malformed IDs** answer the route's 404 code without a store read.
+- **Optional response fields** are always present, `null` when absent.
+- **Rate limit.** `POST /v1/query` (sync and async) is limited per instance by `services.query-api.query-rate-per-minute` (10); beyond it 429 `RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After: 60`.
+
+## POST /v1/query
+
+Answers a collaboration question now (`"execution": "sync"`, the default) or as a job (`"async"`).
+
+Request body (`QueryBody`):
+
+| Field | Type | Notes |
+|---|---|---|
+| `question` | string, required | 1 to `runtime.question.max-chars` (2,000) characters after NFC normalization; no control characters but tab and newline |
+| `constraints` | object or null | `yearFrom`, `yearTo` (integers; `yearFrom` after `yearTo` is 400), `topics`, `institutions`, `venues` (arrays of strings; at most 5 terms of at most 200 characters each, else 422) |
+| `analysis` | object or null | `rankingMetric` (one of `PUBLICATION_COUNT`, `DISTINCT_COAUTHOR_COUNT`, `MAXIMUM_COAUTHORS_ON_ONE_PAPER`, `CROSS_COMMUNITY_DEGREE`, `COMMUNITIES_CONNECTED`, `PARTICIPATION_COEFFICIENT`, `CORENESS`; other values 422), `includeTeamSize`, `includeBridgeMetrics`, `includeCommunities`, `includePaths` (booleans) |
+| `retrieval` | object or null | `graphLimit`, `vectorLimit`, `maxPathDepth` (integers, at least 1; above `retrieval.graph-limit` 100, `vector-limit` 50, `max-path-depth` 6 is 422) |
+| `output` | object | `includeEvidence`, `includeScores`, `includeUncertainty` (booleans, default true); accepted for the spec contract, the answer always carries its evidence IDs |
+| `execution` | string | `sync` (default) or `async` |
+
+An unknown top-level or `output` field is 400 `INVALID_FIELD`; an unknown `constraints` or `analysis` field is 422 `UNSUPPORTED_CONSTRAINT`; an unknown `retrieval` field is 422 `UNSUPPORTED_RETRIEVAL_MODE`. Structured fields override the planner (Phase 9 ruling 4).
+
+Examples: [`query-request.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/query-request.json) (the spec request), [`query-request-minimal.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/query-request-minimal.json), [`query-request-async.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/query-request-async.json).
+
+Response 200, sync (`SyncQueryResponse`), example [`query-response-sync.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/query-response-sync.json):
+
+| Field | Content |
+|---|---|
+| `queryId` | `qry-<32 hex>` |
+| `mode` | `synchronous` (`asynchronous` on `GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}/result`) |
+| `answer` | `summary`, `abstained`, `abstention` (reason and detail), `uncertainty`, `window` (requested and resolved window), `resolutions` (name, decision, researcherId, topShare, candidateCount), `verification` (verified, partiallySupported, unsupported, conflicting), `answerHash` |
+| `claims` | core `AnswerClaim` records: `claimId`, `text`, `evidenceIds`, `verificationStatus`, `uncertainty`, `provenance` |
+| `links` | `explain` (`/v1/explain/<queryId>`) and `evidence` (one `/v1/evidence/<id>` per cited evidence ID) |
+
+Response 202, async (`AsyncQueryResponse`), example [`query-response-async.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/query-response-async.json): `jobId` (`job-<32 hex>`), `queryId`, `mode` (`asynchronous`), `state`, `statusUrl`. If the queue message cannot be sent, the job is moved to FAILED at once and the answer is 502.
+
+Errors: 400, 401, 409, 422, 429, 502, 503, 504, 500 (see the error table).
+
+## GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}
+
+Path parameter `jobId`: `job-<32 hex>`. Response 200 (`JobResponse`): `jobId`, `queryId`, `state` (`QUEUED`, `RUNNING`, `SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED`), `stage`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `startedAt`, `finishedAt` (UTC), `error` (`error`, `message`, `retryable`, when FAILED), `statusUrl`, `resultUrl` (only when SUCCEEDED). The stored request is never returned; jobs expire after `services.ttl.job-hours` (168). Examples: [`job-response.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/job-response.json), [`job-response-failed.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/job-response-failed.json). Errors: 400, 401, 404 `JOB_NOT_FOUND`, 502, 503, 504.
+
+## GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}/result
+
+SUCCEEDED: 200 with the stored answer in the sync response shape, `mode` `asynchronous` (example [`job-result-response.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/job-result-response.json)). QUEUED or RUNNING: 409 `JOB_NOT_FINISHED` (retryable; poll the job). FAILED: the job's own typed error with that code's status (for example 502, 504, or 500). Other errors: 400, 401, 404 `JOB_NOT_FOUND`, 503.
+
+## GET /v1/explain/{queryId}
+
+Path parameter `queryId`: `qry-<32 hex>`. Response 200 (`ExplainResponse`, example [`explain-response.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/explain-response.json)): `queryId`, `runId`, `createdAt`, `auditSchemaVersion`, `mode` (`FAKE` or `LIVE`), `llm` (`STUBBED` or `LIVE`), `plan` (the QueryPlan), `resolutions`, `stages` (outcome and latency), `templateIds`, `templateCalls`, `vectorIndexes`, `embeddingModelVersion`, `promptVersions`, `modelIds`, `graphEvidenceIds`, `semanticEvidenceIds`, `metricVersions`, `graphRunId`, `verification`, `removedClaims` (IDs, kind, status, failed rule names; never their text), `droppedLines`, `abstained`, `abstentionReason`, `llmCalls`, `embeddingCalls`, `budget`, `counters` (`cacheHits` is always 0: the runtime has no cache), `answerHash`. No question, prompt, retrieved, or model text, and no credentials. Errors: 400, 401, 404 `QUERY_NOT_FOUND`, 502, 503, 504.
+
+## GET /v1/evidence/{evidenceId}
+
+Path parameter `evidenceId`: `ev-<32 hex>`. Response 200 (`EvidenceResponse`, example [`evidence-response.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/evidence-response.json)): `evidenceId`, `evidenceType`, `subjectIds`, `workId`, `sourceUri`, `chunkId`, `graphPath`, `score`, `text` (cut to `services.evidence-max-content-chars`, 2,000), `textTruncated`, `textHash` (of the full text), `facts`, `provenance`. The lookup uses the eventually consistent GSI `evidenceById`, so a just-written item can briefly be 404. Errors: 400, 401, 404 `EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND`, 502 (also when the payload fails its integrity check), 503, 504.
+
+## GET /v1/graph/nodes/{nodeId}
+
+Path parameter `nodeId`: a stable node ID, percent-encoded (work IDs contain slashes). Response 200 (`NodeResponse`, example [`node-response.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/node-response.json)): `node` with `nodeId`, `label`, `properties` (provenance included). Errors: 400, 401, 404 `NODE_NOT_FOUND`, 502, 503, 504.
+
+## GET /v1/graph/nodes/{nodeId}/neighbors
+
+Query parameters (each at most once; any other parameter is 400):
+
+| Parameter | Required | Values |
+|---|---|---|
+| `direction` | yes | `in`, `out`, `both` |
+| `depth` | yes | 1 to `graph-read.neighbors-max-depth` (3) |
+| `limit` | yes | 1 to `graph-read.neighbors-max-limit` (200) |
+| `edgeTypes` | yes | comma-separated, non-empty subset of the storage edge labels (`NeptuneContract.Edges`: AUTHORED, COAUTHORED_WITH, PUBLISHED_IN, AFFILIATED_WITH, HAS_TOPIC, MEMBER_OF; [`STORAGE.md`](../modules/storage/STORAGE.md) "Edge files") |
+| `cursor` | no | the opaque `nextCursor` of the previous page, bound to (node, direction, depth, edge types) |
+
+Response 200 (`NeighborsResponse`, example [`neighbors-response.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/neighbors-response.json)): `nodeId`, `direction`, `depth`, `limit`, `edgeTypes`, `nodes`, `edges` (`edgeId`, `edgeType`, `fromId`, `toId`, `properties`), `truncated`, `nextCursor` (present when truncated and a full page was returned). At most `graph-read.max-results` (1,000) nodes over all pages. Errors: 400 `INVALID_FIELD` (missing, repeated, unknown or out-of-bounds parameter; a cursor of another walk; example [`error-invalid-field.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-invalid-field.json)), 401, 404 `NODE_NOT_FOUND`, 502, 503, 504.
+
+## GET /healthz, GET /readyz, GET /metrics
+
+- `/healthz` (public): liveness, depends on no downstream; 200 `{"status":"ok"}` ([`status-healthz.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/status-healthz.json)).
+- `/readyz` (public): probe reads of each downstream the service uses, through the same timeouts and breakers, plus no open breaker; 200 `{"status":"ready"}` ([`status-readyz.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/status-readyz.json)) or 503 `NOT_READY` naming downstreams only (s3, dynamodb, neptune, runtime), never endpoints or configuration.
+- `/metrics` (key required): request counts by route and status, latency percentiles over the last 1,024 requests per route, and counters ([`metrics-response.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/metrics-response.json)).
+
+## Error model
+
+Every error body is `ErrorBody`: `error` (the code), `message` (fixed service text, never a key, body, question or exception message), `traceId`, `requestId`, `retryable`. Status and `retryable` are fixed per code in `ErrorCode`.
+
+| Code | HTTP | Retryable | Raised when | Example file |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| `MALFORMED_JSON` | 400 | no | the body is not valid JSON | [`error-malformed-json.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-malformed-json.json) |
+| `INVALID_FIELD` | 400 | no | a field, header, path or query parameter is invalid | [`error-invalid-field.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-invalid-field.json) |
+| `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` | 400 | no | the body exceeds `services.max-body-bytes` | no example file |
+| `UNAUTHORIZED` | 401 | no | missing or wrong `X-Api-Key` | [`error-unauthorized.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-unauthorized.json) |
+| `FORBIDDEN` | 403 | no | reserved, unused (single-key deployment) | no example file |
+| `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` | 404 | no | no route for the method and path | no example file |
+| `QUERY_NOT_FOUND` | 404 | no | unknown or malformed query ID | [`error-query-not-found.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-query-not-found.json) |
+| `JOB_NOT_FOUND` | 404 | no | unknown, expired or malformed job ID | [`error-job-not-found.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-job-not-found.json) |
+| `NODE_NOT_FOUND` | 404 | no | unknown or malformed node ID | no example file |
+| `EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND` | 404 | no | unknown or malformed evidence ID | [`error-evidence-not-found.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-evidence-not-found.json) |
+| `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` | 409 | no | key reused with another body, or the first request has no stored answer | [`error-idempotency-conflict.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-idempotency-conflict.json) |
+| `JOB_NOT_FINISHED` | 409 | yes | result requested while QUEUED or RUNNING | [`error-job-not-finished.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-job-not-finished.json) |
+| `UNSUPPORTED_CONSTRAINT` | 422 | no | unknown constraint or analysis field, unranked `rankingMetric`, too many or too long terms | [`error-unsupported-constraint.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-unsupported-constraint.json) |
+| `UNSUPPORTED_RETRIEVAL_MODE` | 422 | no | unknown retrieval field, a limit above its maximum | [`error-unsupported-retrieval-mode.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-unsupported-retrieval-mode.json) |
+| `RATE_LIMITED` | 429 | yes | per-instance query rate exceeded (`Retry-After: 60`) | [`error-rate-limited.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-rate-limited.json) |
+| `DOWNSTREAM_FAILURE` | 502 | yes | a workflow stage or store failed, or the job message was not sent | [`error-downstream-failure.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-downstream-failure.json) |
+| `NOT_READY` | 503 | yes | `/readyz` with a failing probe or open breaker | [`error-not-ready.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-not-ready.json) |
+| `CIRCUIT_OPEN` | 503 | yes | a downstream breaker is open | no example file |
+| `OVERLOADED` | 503 | yes | threads and queue full, every workflow slot busy, or a call that could not start in time | no example file |
+| `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED` | 504 | yes | the synchronous deadline (`operations.synchronous-deadline-ms`, 40,000) or a job deadline passed | [`error-deadline-exceeded.json`](../modules/services/openapi/examples/error-deadline-exceeded.json) |
+| `DOWNSTREAM_TIMEOUT` | 504 | yes | a started downstream call timed out | no example file |
+| `INTERNAL_ERROR` | 500 | no | unexpected failure (trace ID, no stack trace) | no example file |
+
+Eight codes have no example file under `openapi/examples/`; their status and retryable flag come from `ErrorCode` in `Enums.scala`.
+
+## Archived live example (window 3)
+
+The smoke test of Nov 19, 2026 ([`operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z.json`](../operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z.json), result PASS) asked "Have Jack J. Dongarra and Yves Robert coauthored a paper?". Its bodies are [`query.json`](../operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z/query.json) (`qry-c08ceb31eb51c6547cda9e522e3d1427`, 2 verified claims) and [`evidence.json`](../operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z/evidence.json) (`ev-927665f3bbac87eb60685e50cfeb8f9b`, `COAUTHOR_EDGE`, 23 joint publications, 2007 to 2021, window `all-years`). The README section "API query examples" shows the commands.

+# ScholarChain architecture
+
+Owner: documentation-engineer. This file shows the two halves of ScholarChain: the build-time agentic factory that produced the repository, and the runtime system that the factory built. Ownership, routing, and gates are defined only in [`docs/agents.md`](agents.md) and [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md); this file references them and does not restate their rules. Per-component details live in the module documents linked below.
+
+## 1. Build time: the agentic software factory
+
+```mermaid
+flowchart TD
+    H["Human architect"] -->|"work order (docs/agent-runs/phase-NN/prompt.md)"| O["Orchestrator (main Claude Code session, no agent file)"]
+    O -->|"delegates one work order"| S["18 subagents (.claude/agents/*.md)"]
+    S -->|"structured final report"| O
+    O -->|"routes BLOCKED-ON and review findings to owners"| S
+    O -->|"assembles agent-report, review-report, test-report, repair-NN"| R["Phase evidence (docs/agent-runs/phase-NN/)"]
+    K["Hooks (.claude/hooks/): guard-dangerous, guard-paths, format-scala, verify-tests-ran, validate-agent-files"] -.->|"PreToolUse, PostToolUse, Stop, SubagentStop"| S
+    P["Permission rules (.claude/settings.json)"] -.->|"allow, ask, deny"| S
+    S -->|"runs"| G["Gates: sbt check, scripts/check.sh, test-engineer, code-reviewer APPROVE"]
+    G -->|"evidence"| R
+    R -->|"review"| H
+    H -->|"human-gate.md, commit.txt, ratification, commit"| R
+```
+
+| Component | Role |
+|---|---|
+| Human architect | Writes each phase work order and its rulings in `docs/agent-runs/phase-NN/prompt.md`, approves plans, applies, cleanups and constitutional changes, runs the cloud windows, and accepts or rejects each phase in `human-gate.md` (spec "Human architect responsibilities"; `docs/agents.md` "Escalation policy and human gates"). |
+| Orchestrator | The main Claude Code session. It has no file under `.claude/agents/`. It delegates to the owning subagent, routes `BLOCKED-ON` results and findings, and writes only the report files it assembles into `docs/agent-runs/` (`docs/agents.md` "Topology"). |
+| Subagents | 18 role files under `.claude/agents/`, one writer per artifact class, no agent-to-agent calls (hub and spoke). The README [agent catalog](../README.md#agent-catalog) lists each one. |
+| Hooks | `guard-dangerous.sh` (PreToolUse on Bash and MCP: destructive cloud, Terraform, Git and secret-reading commands), `guard-paths.sh` (PreToolUse on file tools: ownership map and secret paths), `format-scala.sh` (PostToolUse formatter), `verify-tests-ran.sh` (Stop and SubagentStop: tests must be newer than changed sources), `validate-agent-files.sh` (constitution validator). Their limits are listed in `docs/agents.md` "Hook limitations". |
+| Permission rules | `.claude/settings.json` allow, ask and deny lists; bypass mode disabled. |
+| Gates | `sbt check` (definition of done in `CLAUDE.md`), `scripts/check.sh` (every non-cloud gate), the test-engineer run (Phase 11), and a code-reviewer `APPROVE` before each phase gate. |
+| Phase evidence | `docs/agent-runs/phase-NN/`: prompt, reports, repairs, human gate, accepted commit. Format: [`docs/agent-runs/README.md`](agent-runs/README.md). |
+
+## 2. Runtime: data pipeline, stores, and services
+
+```mermaid
+flowchart LR
+    DS["Dataset scripts (datasets/scripts/)"] --> RAW["S3 raw/ (data bucket)"]
+    RAW --> HD["Hadoop MapReduce on EMR: J01, J02, J03+J04, J05 p0-p2, J06, J10, J07+J08, J09"]
+    HD --> CUR["S3 curated/hadoop/"]
+    CUR --> SP["Spark on EMR (PipelineJob, jobs 1-11)"]
+    SP --> CURS["S3 curated/spark/ (Parquet, load manifest)"]
+    CURS --> CONV["StoreConversionJob, BenchmarkPathJob, CorpusWindowSummaryJob, embedding (Titan V2)"]
+    CONV --> LD["Loaders: LoadS3Cli, LoadNeptuneCli (ECS task in the VPC), LoadDynamoCli, LoadVectorsCli"]
+    LD --> S3["S3: graph/, curated/, semantic-context/, vectors/"]
+    LD --> NEP["Amazon Neptune (openCypher)"]
+    LD --> DDB["Amazon DynamoDB (7 tables)"]
+    LD --> S3V["Amazon S3 Vectors (5 indexes)"]
+    C["Client"] -->|"HTTP, X-Api-Key"| ALB["Application Load Balancer"]
+    ALB --> QA["query-api (ECS Fargate)"]
+    ALB --> GE["graph-evidence-api (ECS Fargate)"]
+    QA -->|"async job"| SQS["SQS job queue and DLQ"]
+    SQS --> WK["worker (ECS Fargate)"]
+    QA --> RAG["RAG runtime (modules/rag), stages 1-10"]
+    WK --> RAG
+    RAG -->|"planner, synthesis"| HK["Bedrock Claude Haiku 4.5"]
+    RAG -->|"query embedding"| TT["Bedrock Titan Text Embeddings V2"]
+    RAG --> NEP
+    RAG --> DDB
+    RAG --> S3V
+    RAG --> S3
+    GE --> NEP
+    GE --> DDB
+    GE --> S3
+```
+
+| Component | What it does | Source |
+|---|---|---|
+| Dataset scripts | Bounded, idempotent fetch and sampling of DBLP (snapshot 2026-10-03), OpenAlex, Crossref and ORCID (snapshots 2026-11-03), DOI-driven from a 46-venue DBLP subset; validation by `bash datasets/scripts/validate.sh`. | [`datasets/dataset-manifest.yaml`](../datasets/dataset-manifest.yaml) |
+| S3 raw | One data bucket per environment, `scholarchain-course-data-<account-id>-us-east-2`, with the prefixes `raw/`, `curated/`, `graph/`, `semantic-context/`, `vectors/`, `reports/`, `logs/`, `runs/` (plus `artifacts/` for jars and image contexts). | [`modules/storage/STORAGE.md`](../modules/storage/STORAGE.md), [`infra/terraform/README.md`](../infra/terraform/README.md) |
+| Hadoop on EMR | Java MapReduce API from Scala 3, driver `scholarchain.hadoop.Pipeline`. J01 DBLP extraction (records never split across mappers), J02 source normalization, J03+J04 exact identifiers, J05 blocking (three passes), J06 candidate join, J10 match decisions (map-only), J07+J08 publication teams and n(n-1)/2 pair expansion, J09 Spark-ready export. Ephemeral cluster per run, `emr-spark-8.0.0`, 1 primary plus up to 2 core `m6g.xlarge`. | [`modules/hadoop/JOBS.md`](../modules/hadoop/JOBS.md), [`scripts/README.md`](../scripts/README.md) `run-bounded-pipeline.sh` |
+| Spark on EMR | `scholarchain.spark.PipelineJob` runs CanonicalGraphJob (jobs 1, 2), ResearcherAnalyticsJob (3, 4, 8), CommunityPathJob (5, 6, 7) and TemporalSummaryExportJob (9, 10, 11): canonical researchers, the temporal weighted coauthorship graph, P_r to W_r, components, bounded paths, label-propagation communities, three bridge measures, k-core, temporal comparison, summaries and the load manifest. On EMR it completed at 25 percent and ran out of memory in job 10 at 50 and 100 percent (P14-D8); the served graph comes from the local full run. | [`modules/spark/JOBS.md`](../modules/spark/JOBS.md), [`docs/evaluation.md`](evaluation.md) sections 5.2 and 7 |
+| Loaders | StoreConversionJob writes the store files (Neptune CSV, DynamoDB item files, evidence objects, embedding candidates); the storage CLIs load them. Neptune runs in private subnets, so `LoadNeptuneCli` runs as a one-off ECS task inside the VPC. `scripts/load-stores.sh` (human-run) loads S3, Neptune, DynamoDB and S3 Vectors in that order. | [`modules/storage/STORAGE.md`](../modules/storage/STORAGE.md), [`scripts/README.md`](../scripts/README.md) `load-stores.sh` |
+| S3 | Authoritative text and evidence objects, run manifests, write-once runtime audits (`logs/rag-runtime/`) and stored answers (`reports/rag-runtime/`). | [`modules/storage/STORAGE.md`](../modules/storage/STORAGE.md) |
+| Neptune | Engine 1.4.8.1 on `db.t4g.medium` (bulk loads ran on `db.r6g.large`, then the instance went back); vertices Researcher, Work, Venue, Institution, Topic, Community; edges AUTHORED, COAUTHORED_WITH, PUBLISHED_IN, AFFILIATED_WITH, HAS_TOPIC, MEMBER_OF. Runtime grant `neptune-db:ReadDataViaQuery` only; parameterized query templates with depth, result and timeout bounds. | [`modules/storage/STORAGE.md`](../modules/storage/STORAGE.md), [`infra/terraform/README.md`](../infra/terraform/README.md) |
+| DynamoDB | Seven on-demand tables, including ResearcherMetricCache (`WINDOW#`, `CHANGE#`, `RANKING#` items), EntityLinkCache, ArtifactRegistry, QuerySession, EvidenceBundle (GSI `evidenceById`) and ServiceState (TTL on `expiresAt`). | [`infra/terraform/README.md`](../infra/terraform/README.md) "DynamoDB" and "IAM summary" |
+| S3 Vectors | One vector bucket with five indexes (`researcher-profile-v1`, `collaboration-community-v1`, `paper-abstract-v1`, `collaboration-path-v1`, `topic-institution-summary-v1`), float32, 1024 dimensions, cosine; long text stays in S3 (`semantic-context/text/`) and the vector metadata keeps a `textRef`. | [`infra/terraform/README.md`](../infra/terraform/README.md) "S3 Vectors", [`modules/storage/STORAGE.md`](../modules/storage/STORAGE.md) |
+| ALB | Public, HTTP on port 80 (no certificate in the course window, so the API key travels in clear text; Phase 12 ruling 11). Routes `/v1/query`, `/v1/jobs/*`, `/v1/explain/*`, `/healthz`, `/readyz` to query-api and `/v1/evidence/*`, `/v1/graph/*` to graph-evidence-api; `/metrics` is not routed. | [`infra/terraform/README.md`](../infra/terraform/README.md) "ECS and the ALB" |
+| query-api | Fargate ARM64 task, port 8080: `POST /v1/query` (sync runs the workflow under the 40 s deadline; async writes a job and one SQS message), jobs, results, explain. | [`modules/services/SERVICES.md`](../modules/services/SERVICES.md), [`docs/api-reference.md`](api-reference.md) |
+| graph-evidence-api | Fargate ARM64 task, port 8081: evidence by ID, graph node, bounded neighbors. No model calls. The only service with a scaling maximum above 1 (target-tracking on CPU). | same |
+| SQS and worker | Job queue (visibility 300 s, redrive to the DLQ after 3 receives) and a worker (port 8082, health and metrics only) that moves jobs QUEUED, RUNNING, SUCCEEDED or FAILED with conditional writes. | [`infra/terraform/README.md`](../infra/terraform/README.md), [`modules/services/openapi.yaml`](../modules/services/openapi.yaml) |
+| RAG runtime | Ten stages: query planning (Haiku, validated against an operation allowlist, rule-planner fallback), researcher resolution (`dominant-name-v1`, abstains on ambiguity), collaboration metrics, path finding, community analysis (all deterministic store reads), semantic context (one Titan embedding, filtered vector queries), evidence building, answer synthesis (Haiku), rule-based claim verification, and a write-once audit. Facts come from the stores; vector similarity only selects descriptive text. | [`modules/rag/RUNTIME.md`](../modules/rag/RUNTIME.md) sections 1 and 2 |
+| Bedrock | `us.anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0` (cross-Region inference profile) for planning and synthesis, `amazon.titan-embed-text-v2:0` for embeddings; throttled per process by `runtime.llm.requests-per-minute`. | [`modules/core/src/main/resources/scholarchain.conf`](../modules/core/src/main/resources/scholarchain.conf), [`infra/terraform/envs/course/course.tfvars`](../infra/terraform/envs/course/course.tfvars) |
+
+All AWS resources come from Terraform under [`infra/terraform/`](../infra/terraform/README.md) in two stages (`runtime_enabled` false, then true); EMR clusters are ephemeral and launched by `scripts/run-bounded-pipeline.sh`. The runtime stage was destroyed on Nov 20, 2026 ([`operations/cleanup/cleanup-20261120T041658Z-runtime/`](../operations/cleanup/cleanup-20261120T041658Z-runtime/record.json)); see the README "Known limitations".

+# ScholarChain data lineage
+
+Owner: documentation-engineer. This file traces a value from a source record to a verified answer claim. The contracts it summarises are authoritative in the module documents: [`modules/hadoop/JOBS.md`](../modules/hadoop/JOBS.md), [`modules/spark/JOBS.md`](../modules/spark/JOBS.md), [`modules/storage/STORAGE.md`](../modules/storage/STORAGE.md) and [`modules/rag/RUNTIME.md`](../modules/rag/RUNTIME.md). Where this file and those documents disagree, they are right. Bucket names are written as `scholarchain-course-data-<account-id>-us-east-2`.
+
+## 1. The chain
+
+```text
+source record (DBLP key, OpenAlex W ID, Crossref DOI, ORCID iD)
+  -> dataset scripts (datasets/scripts/)                 local snapshot files, manifest checksums
+  -> S3 raw/<dataset>/snapshot=<d>/run=<input run>/      uploaded write-once by run-bounded-pipeline.sh
+  -> Hadoop J01..J10 (curated/hadoop/...)                normalized records, rejects, candidates, decisions, pairs
+  -> entity-resolution decisions (J10, policy er-v2)    ACCEPTED, REJECTED, UNRESOLVED, CONFLICT
+  -> Spark jobs 1..11 (curated/spark/...)               canonical tables, coauthor edges, metrics, load manifest
+  -> store conversion (StoreConversionJob and friends)  Neptune CSV, DynamoDB items, evidence objects, vector candidates
+  -> loaders                                            S3, Neptune, DynamoDB, S3 Vectors
+  -> runtime evidence items (ev-...)                    store reads with provenance, stable evidence IDs
+  -> answer claims                                      cite evidence IDs; rule-based verification
+```
+
+## 2. Stage by stage
+
+| Stage | Code and contract | Output location | Provenance carried |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| Source record | [`datasets/dataset-manifest.yaml`](../datasets/dataset-manifest.yaml) | DBLP snapshot 2026-10-03; OpenAlex, Crossref and ORCID snapshots 2026-11-03 (Phase 14 ruling 6) | dataset, snapshot date, checksums in the manifest |
+| Dataset scripts | `datasets/scripts/` (bounded, DOI-driven from the DBLP subset; `make_fraction.sh` for the 25 and 50 percent subsets, rule fraction-v1) | local files under the gitignored `scratch/datasets/`; uploaded by `scripts/run-bounded-pipeline.sh` to `raw/<dataset>/snapshot=<d>/run=<input run>/`, where the input run is the hash of the fraction and the file hashes ([`scripts/README.md`](../scripts/README.md) `run-bounded-pipeline.sh`) | input keys with sha256 and bytes in the pipeline `run.json` |
+| Hadoop MapReduce | J01 DBLP extraction, J02 source normalization, J03+J04 exact identifiers, J05 blocking, J06 candidate join, J10 match decisions, J07+J08 publication teams and pair expansion, J09 Spark-ready export ([`modules/hadoop/JOBS.md`](../modules/hadoop/JOBS.md)) | `<out>/snapshot=<dblp snapshot>/run=<runId>/<job>/<dir>/`, plus `_manifest.json` (`hadoop-run-manifest-v3`) and `_counters/<job>.json`; on EMR `<out>` is `curated/hadoop` | every record carries `Provenance`: source records keep dataset, source record ID and snapshot; teams and pairs keep the DBLP work's source provenance; candidates and decisions are `DERIVED` with the policy version; malformed input goes to `rejects-v1/` with `sourceLocation` and `runId` |
+| Entity resolution | pure functions in [`modules/entity-resolution/`](../modules/entity-resolution/) applied by J05, J06 and J10; policy `er-v2` | Spark reads the J09 `match-decisions/entity_type={RESEARCHER,WORK}/outcome={ACCEPTED,CONFLICT,UNRESOLVED}/`; REJECTED decisions are not read, and only ACCEPTED decisions link records ([`modules/spark/JOBS.md`](../modules/spark/JOBS.md) "Inputs") | decision, features, rules fired, confidence, policy version, run ID |
+| Spark | `PipelineJob`: CanonicalGraphJob (jobs 1, 2), ResearcherAnalyticsJob (3, 4, 8), CommunityPathJob (5, 6, 7), TemporalSummaryExportJob (9, 10, 11) ([`modules/spark/JOBS.md`](../modules/spark/JOBS.md)) | `<out>/snapshot=<snapshot>/run=<stage run>/<table>/`; the export stage writes `export/load-manifest.json` | every row has `schemaVersion` and a `provenance` struct (`DERIVED`, `sourceRecordId` = the row key, snapshot, `runId` = the stage run that wrote the row, policy version); canonical IDs resolve to source records through `canonical/mention-map` and `canonical/work-map` |
+| Store conversion | `StoreConversionJob`, `BenchmarkPathJob`, `CorpusWindowSummaryJob`, the embedding runs ([`modules/spark/JOBS.md`](../modules/spark/JOBS.md), [`modules/storage/STORAGE.md`](../modules/storage/STORAGE.md)) | `graph/neptune/snapshot=<d>/run=<r>/{vertices,edges}/`, `curated/dynamodb/...`, `curated/coauthor-evidence/...` (edges with more than 20 works), `semantic-context/...`, `vectors/backup/...`; `runs/store-conversion/.../manifest.json` (`store-run-manifest-v1`) | every Neptune element keeps `runId` and `snapshot` of the Phase 6 stage run that computed it, `schemaVersion`, and (edges) `policyVersion`; every manifest entry has sha256, bytes and `sourceRef` (`<table>@<run>`) |
+| Loaders | `LoadS3Cli`, `LoadNeptuneCli` (ECS task in the VPC), `LoadDynamoCli`, `LoadVectorsCli`, run by `scripts/load-stores.sh` | the data bucket, Neptune, seven DynamoDB tables, five S3 Vectors indexes | write-once objects (a different body under an existing key is a conflict); S3 objects checked against the manifest sha256 (Phase 14 ruling 40: 609 of 609) |
+| Runtime evidence | stages 3 to 7 of [`modules/rag/RUNTIME.md`](../modules/rag/RUNTIME.md) | evidence items in the answer's bundle; `EvidenceBundle` items in DynamoDB; audit object `logs/rag-runtime/snapshot=<d>/run=<runtime run>/<queryId>.json` (section 14) | `StableIds.evidenceId(type, subjectIds, runId)`; `sourceUri` is a store pointer (`neptune:<element id>`, `dynamodb:<Table>/<pk>/<sk>`, an S3 key); `facts` hold the stored values; `provenance` is the stored element's |
+| Answer claim | stages 8 and 9 (synthesis, verification) | `claims[]` of the answer | each claim lists `evidenceIds`; the verifier checks that they exist and that every asserted value equals a cited fact; claim provenance names the runtime run and the model |
+
+The served graph is the local full Spark run, not an EMR run: Spark on EMR completed at 25 percent and ran out of memory in job 10 at 50 and 100 percent (P14-D8; [`docs/agent-runs/phase-14/human-gate.md`](agent-runs/phase-14/human-gate.md)). The served runs are named in [`infra/terraform/envs/course/course.tfvars`](../infra/terraform/envs/course/course.tfvars): graph and items `run-1d6a86491812ff94887270c1ab885e69` (store conversion), vectors `run-b28edbd40f932f0342c05d5b6f57b46c`, window summary `run-6fbad498beb0d8f570df67665fb97544`.
+
+## 3. Traced example: evidence `ev-927665f3bbac87eb60685e50cfeb8f9b`
+
+The window 3 smoke test asked benchmark question B07, "Have Jack J. Dongarra and Yves Robert coauthored a paper?" ([`operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z.json`](../operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z.json), result PASS). Each step below names the record it was read from.
+
+### 3.1 Answer claim
+
+From [`operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z/query.json`](../operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z/query.json):
+
+| Field | Value |
+|---|---|
+| `queryId` | `qry-c08ceb31eb51c6547cda9e522e3d1427` |
+| claim | `claim-1`: "Jack J. Dongarra and Yves Robert coauthored 23 joint publications from 2007 to 2021." |
+| `evidenceIds` | `ev-927665f3bbac87eb60685e50cfeb8f9b` |
+| `verificationStatus` | `VERIFIED` |
+| claim provenance | `sourceDataset` RUNTIME, `runId` `run-c9f26a9ecfe83c96526d50d79d99fd67` (the window 3 runtime run, also in the release record), `modelVersion` `us.anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0` |
+| identity lines | Jack J. Dongarra linked to `scr:canon-v1.er-v2:6de4012bbe8608f1d49d18cf9a40a763` by `dominant-name-v1` (0.9576 of 377 works among 17 researchers with this display name); Yves Robert linked to `scr:canon-v1.er-v2:da6e3bdff14745638f3f5425f0133388` (0.9775 of 222 works among 6) |
+| verification counts | verified 2, unsupported 3 (removed from the answer, kept in the verification report) |
+
+### 3.2 Evidence item
+
+From [`operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z/evidence.json`](../operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z/evidence.json):
+
+| Field | Value |
+|---|---|
+| `evidenceType` | `COAUTHOR_EDGE` |
+| `subjectIds` | the two researcher IDs above |
+| `sourceUri` | `neptune:cae-94049b45586a9d41217209d71135c559` |
+| facts | `edgeId` `cae-94049b45586a9d41217209d71135c559`; window `all-years` (2000 to 2026); `jointPublicationCount` 23; `firstCollaborationYear` 2007; `lastCollaborationYear` 2021; 23 `supportingWorkIds`; `supportingWorkIdsRef` `curated/coauthor-evidence/snapshot=2026-10-03/run=run-1d6a86491812ff94887270c1ab885e69/window=all-years/cae-94049b45586a9d41217209d71135c559.json` |
+| provenance | `sourceDataset` DERIVED, `sourceRecordId` the edge ID, `snapshot` 2026-10-03, `runId` `run-10ceed31b3f602bb998eae7c00929ae9`, `policyVersion` `graph-v1` |
+
+The claim's three numbers (23, 2007, 2021) are the stored facts `jointPublicationCount`, `firstCollaborationYear` and `lastCollaborationYear`; the verifier binds each written number to one of them (RUNTIME.md section 12, `asserted-values` and `numbers-in-text`). The window 2 smoke query `qry-41d3b96234f65188482930ad99486165` cited the same evidence ID (Phase 14 ruling 45), because an evidence ID depends only on the type, the subjects and the source run (RUNTIME.md section 10).
+
+### 3.3 Graph edge and store files
+
+- **Neptune edge.** `cae-94049b45586a9d41217209d71135c559` is a `COAUTHORED_WITH` edge. Its ID is the Phase 6 edge ID, `StableIds.coauthorEdgeId(start, end, windowStartYear, windowEndYear, policyVersion)` with policy `graph-v1`, and `:START_ID` is the smaller researcher ID ([`modules/storage/STORAGE.md`](../modules/storage/STORAGE.md) "Edge files").
+- **Bulk-load file.** The edge came from the store-conversion run `run-1d6a86491812ff94887270c1ab885e69` (`graph/neptune/snapshot=2026-10-03/run=run-1d6a86491812ff94887270c1ab885e69/edges/coauthored-with/`), the `runtime_graph_run` of `course.tfvars`. Window 3 loaded it with Neptune load `6113dd40` (LOAD_COMPLETED, Phase 15 ruling 10(b)).
+- **Evidence object.** With 23 works the edge is above the 20-work inline limit, so the edge holds `supportingWorkIdsRef`, the S3 key above, outside the `graph/` prefix (STORAGE.md "COAUTHORED_WITH evidence"). The Phase 7 validator traced every `COAUTHORED_WITH` edge of this run to Work vertices authored by both endpoints: 1,590,754 edges, 0 `EVIDENCE_WORK_MISSING`, 0 `EVIDENCE_NOT_AUTHORED` ([`docs/agent-runs/phase-07/human-gate.md`](agent-runs/phase-07/human-gate.md)).
+- **Store copy.** `LoadS3Cli` uploaded the store-conversion objects in window 2, 609 of 609 matching the manifest by SHA-256 (Phase 14 ruling 40). The data bucket was kept by the runtime cleanup ([`operations/cleanup/cleanup-20261120T041658Z-runtime/`](../operations/cleanup/cleanup-20261120T041658Z-runtime/record.json)).
+
+### 3.4 Spark run
+
+- **Stage run.** The evidence provenance `runId` `run-10ceed31b3f602bb998eae7c00929ae9` is the Phase 6 stage run of jobs 1 and 2 (CanonicalGraphJob), which wrote `graph/coauthor-edges`. The Phase 6 gate lists it with the other stage runs: `run-d7865ad3f5f8195546c1dab817328ee5` (jobs 3, 4, 8), `run-83b496169099a614ec519133077a1993` (jobs 5 to 7), `run-0375535e0174b7330b18a78988c07211` (jobs 9 to 11, the export), in pipeline run `run-de1f982ae8f83848b4f869d8718f5e3d`, code version `src-93bc726cd50a` ([`docs/agent-runs/phase-06/human-gate.md`](agent-runs/phase-06/human-gate.md)). The manifests are in `docs/agent-runs/phase-06/evidence/` (`pipeline-manifest.json`, `load-manifest.json`, `stage-manifest-*.json`).
+- **Edge semantics.** Job 2 maps Hadoop pair candidates to canonical researchers and works, drops self-pairs, orders each pair, and aggregates per window: `jointPublicationCount` is the number of distinct canonical works, the first and last years are the minimum and maximum pair year, and `representativeWorkIds` is the complete sorted list ([`modules/spark/JOBS.md`](../modules/spark/JOBS.md) "Coauthorship graph").
+- **Researcher IDs.** `scr:canon-v1.er-v2:<hash>` is `StableIds.researcherId` over the canonical policy `canon-v1`, the match policy `er-v2` and the smallest member mention ID. Every member mention, with its source dataset and source record ID, is a row of `canonical/mention-map` of the same stage run.
+- **Store conversion input.** `StoreConversionJob` read every Phase 6 table through the export's `load-manifest.json` and the benchmark path run `run-ee4ffd21aa20dbe4c078a4be96fe8c9f` ([`modules/storage/STORAGE.md`](../modules/storage/STORAGE.md) line 5; [`docs/agent-runs/phase-07/human-gate.md`](agent-runs/phase-07/human-gate.md)).
+
+### 3.5 Hadoop run and source records
+
+- **Hadoop run.** The Phase 6 run read the Hadoop run `run-035e75abfa6798c938cbd534273dbd8d`, the Phase 5 repair-02 decisions under policy `er-v2` ([`docs/agent-runs/phase-06/human-gate.md`](agent-runs/phase-06/human-gate.md); Phase 5 records in [`docs/agent-runs/phase-05/`](agent-runs/phase-05/)). In that run, J07+J08 expanded each DBLP work into its coauthor pair candidates, each with work ID, year, author positions and the work's source provenance, and J09 exported them with the match decisions ([`modules/hadoop/JOBS.md`](../modules/hadoop/JOBS.md)).
+- **Source records.** The 23 supporting work IDs are DBLP work IDs. J01 maps a DBLP record with key `k` to `workId = dblp:k` and `sourceRecordId = k`. For example `dblp:conf/ipps/DongarraPRSV07` is the record with key `conf/ipps/DongarraPRSV07` in the DBLP snapshot of 2026-10-03, and `dblp:journals/pc/HerrmannBHMRD16` is `journals/pc/HerrmannBHMRD16`.
+
+### 3.6 What this trace did not open
+
+The evidence object, the Neptune CSV files, the Phase 6 manifests and the Hadoop run directory were not opened for this document. The trace rests on the records cited above. The Hadoop and Spark run directories of the local runs are under the gitignored `scratch/`; their store-ready outputs are in the kept data bucket.

+## 4. Part A: live runtime (window 3)
+
+A0 to A4 have their original audio; the source holds no voiceover text for them. A0 is the opening, A1 the deployed resources, A2 a complete query through the API, A3 its evidence and explain responses, A4 a Neptune graph query. A2 to A5 were recorded in one Terminal session and share the query ID and evidence ID of A2.
+
+### A5. S3 Vectors source pointers (item 13)
+
+**On screen:** the `jq` output of the explain record (`queryId`, `embeddingModelVersion`, `embeddingCalls`, `vectorIndexes`, `semanticEvidenceIds`), then one semantic evidence item resolved through the evidence endpoint.
+
+**Voiceover:**
+
+[As the first output appears] "Now the semantic side. Each answer's explain record lists how semantic context was used. For this question, the runtime embedded the text once with the Titan text embedding model, and searched two S3 Vectors indexes: researcher profiles and paper abstracts. These are the IDs of the semantic evidence it kept."
+
+[As the evidence item appears] "Resolving one of those IDs shows a profile item. It carries the vector key it was found under, and a pointer to its source text in S3, with a hash, so text that has been changed since it was embedded is dropped. Semantic items are kept apart from graph facts: they can add description to an answer, but they can never verify a number, an edge or a path."
+
+### A6. DynamoDB records (item 12)
+
+**On screen:** the DynamoDB console: `scholarchain-course-QuerySession` items, then `scholarchain-course-EvidenceBundle`, then `scholarchain-course-ResearcherMetricCache`.
+
+**Voiceover:**
+
+[QuerySession table and an item] "DynamoDB holds the operational state. Every query writes a session record: the query ID, the runtime run, the model and prompt versions, the timings, and references to the answer's evidence. It deliberately stores no question text."
+
+[EvidenceBundle] "The evidence bundle of each answer is stored item by item, so any evidence ID in an answer can be looked up later through the evidence endpoint. These items expire after a set time."
+
+[ResearcherMetricCache] "And this cache holds the Spark-computed metrics of each researcher per time window, such as distinct coauthors, weighted degree, community and k-core, each with provenance back to the Spark run that produced it. The runtime reads these instead of recomputing them."
+
+### A7. CloudWatch dashboard and alarms (item 19)
+
+**On screen:** the `scholarchain-course-runtime` dashboard over 6 hours, then the Alarms list.
+
+**Voiceover:**
+
+[Dashboard] "This is the runtime dashboard. It shows CPU for each of the three services, load balancer requests, latency percentiles and errors, healthy targets, the dead-letter queue depth, and Neptune's CPU. The large Neptune spike earlier in the day is the bulk load of about 34 million graph records."
+
+[Alarms] "Alarms cover load balancer errors, unhealthy targets, high CPU on each service and on Neptune, and any message in the dead-letter queue. The two TargetTracking alarms were created by the autoscaling policy itself; they are what drives scale-out and scale-in, which I'll show shortly."
+
+### A8. Failure and recovery (item 21)
+
+**On screen:** the listing of `experiments/results/exp-f-failure-recovery-v1/20261118T062000Z`, its `run-record.json`, then the end of `stdout.txt`.
+
+**Voiceover:**
+
+[File listing] "This is experiment F, failure and recovery, run in the previous cloud window. Its raw results are committed and read-only."
+
+[run-record.json] "The run record ties the result to the exact commit, the manifest and its checksum, the time limits and the exit code."
+
+[stdout] "I injected a job that always fails. The worker retried it three times, then SQS moved the message to the dead-letter queue and the alarm fired. After I redrove the message, the job completed exactly once, and replaying the same request with the same idempotency key returned the same answer instead of running it again."
+
+### A9. Guard hooks blocking dangerous commands (item 4)
+
+**On screen:** the Claude Code sidebar, the probe prompt, and the three blocked commands with the hook's messages.
+
+**Voiceover:**
+
+[Prompt pasted] "The agents work under executable policy, not just instructions. Here I ask Claude Code to run three dangerous commands, each one safe by construction: a dry-run recursive delete, and two commands that name resources that don't exist."
+
+[First block] "A recursive S3 delete is blocked before it runs, by the guard-dangerous hook."
+
+[Second block] "Terminating an EMR cluster is blocked too."
+
+[Third block] "And setting a service to a hundred tasks is refused as a scale change beyond the approved bounds, which needs human approval. All three were stopped by the hook before anything reached AWS. The settings file also has deny rules for destructive commands, and the gate script self-tests these hooks with 304 cases."
+
+### A10. Scale-out and scale-in (item 20)
+
+**On screen:** the experiment's output lines, the scaling-activities table, the current task count, the ECS service Events tab, then the dashboard from 6:10 to 7:00 PM. The live-clip version of A10 was not used.
+
+**Voiceover:**
+
+[Experiment output] "This is the elasticity experiment, run earlier that evening. graph-evidence-api has a target-tracking policy on average CPU, with one to two tasks. For ten minutes, eight parallel clients sent graph and evidence requests: almost 22,000 requests, about 37 per second, with zero errors and a 95th-percentile latency of 683 milliseconds. One task was running before the load, two at the end of it, and one again after the observation period."
+
+[Scaling activities] "Application Auto Scaling recorded both decisions: at 6:24 PM, five minutes into the load, it raised the desired count to two, and at 6:46 PM, about seventeen minutes after the load stopped, it set it back to one. Nobody changed the task count by hand."
+
+[Task count] "The service is back at its normal size: one task desired, one running."
+
+[ECS Events] "ECS's own event log shows the full cycle. The high-CPU alarm went into alarm, the desired count rose to two, and ECS started a second task and registered it with the load balancer. Later the low-CPU alarm fired, and ECS deregistered that same task, drained its connections and stopped it." [If the morning's failed deployment row is visible:] "The failed deployment that morning is from before the API key was set; the resumed deployment completed."
+
+[Dashboard] "Here is the service's CPU during the experiment. For this run the target was 15 percent, a declared experiment setting: in the previous window the service never got near the normal 60 percent, because Neptune was the bottleneck. Neptune was at about 97 percent CPU throughout, so the scaling shows the mechanism working, not extra capacity; that's discussed in the evaluation."
+
+Sources for the narrated figures: Phase 15 ruling 10(e) (21,962 requests in 600 s, 0 errors, p95 683 ms, desired count 1 to 2 at 18:24:01 CST and 2 to 1 at 18:46:39 CST) Phase 15 rulings 12(e) and 13(c) (Neptune CPU about 97 percent in both phases; the throughput difference is not attributed to the second task); [`docs/evaluation.md`](evaluation.md) section 5.
+
+### A11. Cleanup evidence (item 22)
+
+**On screen:** the end of the cleanup apply log, the count of managed resources kept, the empty post-cleanup checks, then the empty ECS, Neptune, DynamoDB and Load Balancer console pages.
+
+**Voiceover:**
+
+[Apply log] "The runtime is removed through the same reviewed path that created it. The cleanup script wrote a plan of 89 resources to destroy, I approved it by its checksum, and only then did it destroy them: 89 destroyed, nothing else changed."
+
+[Kept count and checks] "43 managed resources remain, each kept on purpose, and the checks find no Neptune cluster, no ECS cluster, no DynamoDB tables, no load balancer and no running EMR cluster."
+
+[Console pages] "The consoles agree: nothing billable from the runtime is left running. What remains is the network, the encrypted data bucket with the pipeline outputs and evidence, the container registry, the image build project, the EMR roles, and log groups that expire on their own. They cost almost nothing at rest and are needed to reproduce the pipeline. The final cleanup, including the data bucket, happens after grading."

+# ScholarChain runbook
+
+Owner: documentation-engineer. Operating procedures for the course environment (us-east-2). The script contracts are authoritative in [`scripts/README.md`](../scripts/README.md) and in each script's `--help`; who may run what is set by [`docs/agents.md`](agents.md) ("Exceptions and clarifications", item 8, and "Escalation policy and human gates"). None of the cloud commands below was replayed for this document: the runtime was destroyed on Nov 20, 2026 and only the 43 base resources remain ([`operations/cleanup/cleanup-20261120T041658Z-runtime/`](../operations/cleanup/cleanup-20261120T041658Z-runtime/record.json)). Each command names its source.
+
+## 1. Before any cloud step
+
+- Clean tree. `plan.sh`, `deploy.sh`, `package.sh`, `run-bounded-pipeline.sh`, `load-stores.sh`, `run-experiment.sh` and `cleanup.sh` stop with exit 3 on any change outside `operations/` and `experiments/results/`, untracked files included; `deploy.sh` also needs HEAD equal to the plan's commit ([`scripts/README.md`](../scripts/README.md) "Conventions", `deploy.sh`).
+- `aws login` immediately before each long script. Every script prints `==> TARGET account=... region=... profile=...` first; check it. `--region` accepts only `us-east-2`.
+- Exit codes: 0 success, 1 a gate failed, 2 usage or missing tool, 3 precondition, 4 approval mismatch, 5 waiting for a human action, 6 cloud timeout.
+- In window 3 every script was human-run (Phase 15 ruling 3). The window time and cost caps are set by ruling before each window (Phase 14 ruling 26; Phase 15 ruling 3).
+
+## 2. Deployment, in the corrected window order
+
+From [`scripts/README.md`](../scripts/README.md) "The flow" (window 3, the last deployed release). The order is apply, secret, loads, then `deploy.sh --resume`, because query-api and the worker exit at startup until `load-stores.sh` has uploaded the window-summary object (Phase 14 ruling 39).
+
+```text
+  human:  scripts/plan.sh --runtime-enabled true                                         -> sign the sha256
+  human:  scripts/deploy.sh --plan-file <plan> --approved-plan-sha256 <sha> --package-record <rec>
+  human:  sets the API key secret value; backs up the state
+  human:  Neptune scholarchain-course-graph-1 to db.r6g.large (--apply-immediately; temporary drift from
+          Terraform, Phase 14 rulings 42 and 43: the bulk load needs more than db.t4g.medium's memory)
+  human:  scripts/load-stores.sh --store-run <run> --vectors-run <run>
+            --skip s3-store-conversion,s3-embed,s3-window-summary --neptune-timeout-minutes 180
+  human:  Neptune back to db.t4g.medium, waits until available (Terraform and the instance agree again)
+  human:  the same deploy.sh command + --resume
+  human:  Neptune warm-up (Phase 14 rulings 46 and 47: three sequential neighbors GETs for each of the two
+          researchers, API key on standard input), then scripts/smoke-test.sh
+  human:  demonstration recording; scripts/run-experiment.sh --manifest experiments/manifests/exp-d-scale-v1.json
+  human:  scripts/archive-evidence.sh; scripts/cleanup.sh --stage runtime; backs up the state
+```
+
+Notes from the window records:
+
+- `plan.sh` needs `TF_VAR_alarm_email` (empty was used in windows 2 and 3) and `AWS_PROFILE`; it writes the plan, its sha256 and `summary.md` under `scratch/plans/<stamp>-<commit12>/` and copies the summary to [`operations/plans/`](../operations/plans/). The human writes an approval record beside it (example: [`operations/plans/20261119T173346Z-c5428063732a-APPROVED.txt`](../operations/plans/20261119T173346Z-c5428063732a-APPROVED.txt)).
+- Pass `--plan-file` as printed by `plan.sh`. `deploy.sh` exits 4 on a checksum mismatch and stops with exit 5 until the API key secret has a value; the human sets it by the stdin pattern in [`infra/terraform/README.md`](../infra/terraform/README.md) (Phase 14 ruling 36).
+- Start `load-stores.sh` only after `describe-db-instances` shows `scholarchain-course-graph-1` as `db.r6g.large`, available, with no pending modified values. Once `==> NEPTUNE load=<id>` has printed, never rerun `load-stores.sh` without `neptune` in `--skip`; record the load ID and check it with manual status tasks first (Phase 15 ruling 7(c)).
+- Recorded window 3 outcome: plan `978ce305...` 89 to add, 0 to change, 0 to destroy; Neptune load 6113dd40 LOAD_COMPLETED with 34,052,689 records; DynamoDB 876,228 items; 13,023 vectors; `deploy.sh --resume` exit 0 with digest `sha256:5c751b1e...` on all three services (Phase 15 ruling 10; [`operations/releases/release-20261119T233215Z-c5428063732a.json`](../operations/releases/release-20261119T233215Z-c5428063732a.json)).
+
+## 3. Neptune warm-up and smoke test
+
+- **Warm-up.** Three sequential `GET /v1/graph/nodes/<researcherId>/neighbors` requests (depth 1, limit 50, edge type `COAUTHORED_WITH`) for each of the two B07 researchers, `scr:canon-v1.er-v2:6de4012bbe8608f1d49d18cf9a40a763` and `scr:canon-v1.er-v2:da6e3bdff14745638f3f5425f0133388`, with the API key on standard input as in the README API examples (`curl --config -`). Parameters: [`docs/api-reference.md`](api-reference.md). Expected: every response 200. In window 2 the high-degree node took 3.40 s cold, then 0.55 to 0.60 s (Phase 14 ruling 47); in window 3 the warm-up returned two 504s, then 200 (Phase 15 ruling 10(d)). The exact curl lines of the warm-ups are not recorded in the repository.
+- **Smoke test** (source: [`scripts/README.md`](../scripts/README.md) `smoke-test.sh`):
+
+  ```bash
+  scripts/smoke-test.sh --profile <profile> --dry-run
+  scripts/smoke-test.sh --profile <profile>
+  ```
+
+  Six checks: `/healthz` 200, `/readyz` 200, a sync B07 query with a fresh Idempotency-Key returning a `qry-` ID and a claim with an evidence ID, that evidence fetched, the same POST replaying the same queryId and answerHash, and a POST without `X-Api-Key` answering 401. Record: `operations/releases/smoke-<stamp>.json` and the bodies in `smoke-<stamp>/`. The last PASS: [`operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z.json`](../operations/releases/smoke-20261119T235713Z.json). Never rerun after a failure without a ruling; a failure goes to the rollback gate (section 5).
+
+## 4. Common failures
+
+| Symptom | Cause | Action | Record |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| A long script fails mid-run with a credential error (for example `credential_process` exit 255) | The `aws login` session expired during the run. In window 2 cleanup apply 1 destroyed 77 resources, then failed while waiting on the Neptune delete. Earlier, a live embedding run stopped on "The security token included in the request is expired". | Run `aws login` immediately before each long script. After a partial apply: confirm no terraform process and no lock file, back up the state, run a fresh plan, check it, apply it. The embedding client now refreshes credentials and retries once on an expired token. | Phase 14 ruling 48; [`docs/agent-runs/phase-07/human-gate.md`](agent-runs/phase-07/human-gate.md) |
+| Smoke query 502 `DOWNSTREAM_FAILURE`, or neighbors 504 `DOWNSTREAM_TIMEOUT`, right after a resize | Cold Neptune buffer cache on `db.t4g.medium`: graph queries hit `TimeLimitExceededException` at the query timeout. | Warm up (section 3) before the smoke test and any load test. If the smoke test fails again with a Neptune timeout, the window 2 ruling allowed a resize to `db.r6g.large`, reported as a deviation. | Phase 14 rulings 44 to 47 |
+| Neptune bulk load ends `LOAD_CANCELLED_DUE_TO_ERRORS` with `OUT_OF_MEMORY_ERROR` | The bulk load of this graph needs more memory than `db.t4g.medium` (4 GiB). | Resize to `db.r6g.large` with `--apply-immediately`, start one new load of the same run (already-loaded elements are duplicates and are not changed), poll status until LOAD_COMPLETED, resize back to `db.t4g.medium` before measurements. | Phase 14 rulings 42 and 43 |
+| `plan.sh` or `cleanup.sh` cannot acquire the state lock | An interrupted run (Ctrl+C during `plan.sh`) left a stale lock and an empty plan directory. | Confirm that no process holds the state, then clear the lock. The exact command used is not recorded. | Phase 14 ruling 38 |
+| `load-stores.sh` step `s3-store-conversion` exits 1 after every object was uploaded | `LoadS3Cli` verified the run by downloading every object under a fixed 120 s per-call timeout; laptop downloads of the 200 MB DynamoDB files were too slow. | Check the objects against the manifest with S3's stored SHA-256 (HEAD, no download), then continue without the S3 steps (`--skip s3-store-conversion,s3-embed,s3-window-summary`). A HEAD-checksum verify in `LoadS3Cli` is a storage-engineer follow-up. | Phase 14 ruling 40 |
+| `load-stores.sh` cannot find the Neptune load ID | The loader logs the AWS SDK at DEBUG, and the first page of `get-log-events` missed the `started load` line. | Fixed: the script reads with `filter-log-events` across every page and re-reads up to 3 times. Session-token lines are dropped from records (see [`docs/threat-model.md`](threat-model.md)). | Phase 14 ruling 41; Phase 15 repair-01 part A |
+| `deploy.sh --resume` exits 1; query-api and the worker exit at startup with `OBJECT_MISSING` | The window-summary object was not yet in the data bucket (resume before loads). | Run `load-stores.sh` first, then `--resume` (section 2). | Phase 14 ruling 39 |
+| Apply fails because the secret name `scholarchain-course/api-key` is taken | The previous cleanup left the secret in its 7-day recovery window; a new secret cannot take a name pending deletion. | Before the plan, restore and then force-delete the secret (human). The current secret is pending deletion until about Nov 26 to 27, 2026. | Phase 14 gate; Phase 15 ruling 3; [`docs/agent-runs/phase-15/human-gate.md`](agent-runs/phase-15/human-gate.md) |
+| A script stops with exit 3, dirty tree | A change or untracked file outside `operations/` and `experiments/results/` (for example an agent report under `docs/agent-runs/` during a window). | Commit or move the file before the plan; nothing is committed between the plan and cleanup. Hiding a file with `.git/info/exclude` was used only under rulings 40 and 41 and removed afterwards. | [`scripts/README.md`](../scripts/README.md) "Conventions"; Phase 14 ruling 5 |
+| `deploy.sh` or `cleanup.sh` passes its checksum gate but terraform cannot load the plan | A relative `--plan-file` (terraform runs with `-chdir`). | `deploy.sh` now resolves the path (P14-D1); `cleanup.sh` still needs the absolute path. | Phase 14 rulings 11 and 12 |
+
+## 5. Rollback
+
+From [`scripts/README.md`](../scripts/README.md) `rollback.sh`:
+
+```bash
+scripts/rollback.sh --list
+scripts/rollback.sh --to-release operations/releases/release-<stamp>-<sha12>.json --reason "<text>" --dry-run
+scripts/rollback.sh --to-release operations/releases/release-<stamp>-<sha12>.json --reason "<text>"
+```
+
+- It moves query-api, graph-evidence-api and the worker together to the image of a known-good release record (new task definition revisions, no Terraform apply), waits until stable, and checks digests and runtime run IDs. Record: `operations/releases/rollback-<stamp>.json`.
+- Who: the deploy-engineer for its own failing in-flight deployment (inside `/deploy`); the incident-responder for an already-live revision through [`/rollback`](../.claude/commands/rollback.md). Each AWS mutation is approved at the permission prompt. A failing ECS deployment is first rolled back by the ECS deployment circuit breaker.
+- Terraform state still names the newer image until `image_tag` in `course.tfvars` is changed and applied; the next plan shows that drift. Afterwards run the smoke test (section 3).
+- The window rulings and gates of Phases 14 and 15 record no rollback run.
+
+## 6. Incident escalation
+
+- Open [`/incident`](../.claude/commands/incident.md) with the symptom, environment and IDs. The orchestrator delegates to the incident-responder; diagnosis is read-only first.
+- Autonomous recovery is limited to `scripts/rollback.sh` to the last known-good release, an ECS task restart, and an ECS desired count within 1 to 4, each approved at the permission prompt ([`docs/agents.md`](agents.md) "Escalation policy and human gates").
+- Data repair or deletion, schema rollback, Terraform, IAM, KMS, network, EMR resizing or termination, scaling beyond bounds and any security finding stop and escalate to the human architect. Suspected secret exposure: stop immediately without echoing the value.
+- The report goes to `docs/incidents/<UTC-date>-<slug>.md` (incident-responder). During the windows, mid-window decisions were written by the human to [`operations/rulings/`](../operations/rulings/) because HEAD was pinned.
+
+## 7. Cleanup (human only)
+
+`scripts/cleanup.sh` is run by the human only; agents are denied by permission rules and blocked by `guard-dangerous.sh`. Source: [`scripts/README.md`](../scripts/README.md) `cleanup.sh`.
+
+```bash
+aws login   # immediately before
+TF_VAR_alarm_email=<address or empty> scripts/cleanup.sh --stage infra
+scripts/cleanup.sh --plan-file "$PWD/scratch/plans/cleanup-<stamp>-infra/plan.tfplan" --confirm <sha256>
+# after the instructor approves the evidence archive location
+TF_VAR_alarm_email=<address or empty> scripts/cleanup.sh --stage final --evidence-archive <location>
+scripts/cleanup.sh --plan-file "$PWD/scratch/plans/cleanup-<stamp>-final/plan.tfplan" --confirm <sha256>
+```
+
+- The first invocation writes the inventory, the full plan, every resource destroyed and kept with its reason, and the plan sha256; nothing changes. The second applies only that plan, takes the second inventory, and writes `operations/cleanup/cleanup-<stamp>-<stage>/record.json`.
+- `--plan-file` must be the absolute path: `cleanup.sh` passes it unchanged to terraform, which runs with `-chdir` (Phase 14 ruling 11).
+- Stages: `runtime` destroys the stores, services, ALB, secret, alarms and runtime roles; `infra` the network, EMR roles and groups and CodeBuild; `final` everything in state except what survives because the data bucket and ECR repository are not empty. Ephemeral EMR clusters are listed, never terminated.
+- Back up the Terraform state before and after.
+- State now: the `runtime` stage ran on Nov 20, 2026 (89 destroyed, 43 kept). The final cleanup (`--stage infra`, then `--stage final`) is planned for December 15, 2026 (Phase 17 ruling 8).

An account's quota can be lower or higher than the documented default (see the Bedrock and EC2 pages below), so the values in section 1 are the ones that bound this deployment.

+## 2. How the implementation stays inside them
+
+| Limit | Control | Record |
+|---|---|---|
+| Haiku 10 requests per minute | The code throttles every Bedrock call; the configured cap is 8 per minute (`runtime.llm.requests-per-minute`). On ECS, query-api and the worker each get 4 per minute (`llm_requests_per_minute = 4`), and each runs at most 1 task, so the two together stay at 8 (`haiku_calls_per_minute_cap = 8`). The override variable is `SCHOLARCHAIN_LLM_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE`. graph-evidence-api makes no model call. | Phase 10 ruling 10; Phase 12 work order 4; [`course.tfvars`](../infra/terraform/envs/course/course.tfvars) |
+| Titan 60 requests per minute | The embedding throttle is 50 per minute; the live embedding of 13,023 vectors ran at 50 per minute with 0 rejected. A question makes at most one embedding call. | Phase 7 gate; [`modules/rag/RUNTIME.md`](../modules/rag/RUNTIME.md) section 9 |
+| 16 vCPUs | `run-bounded-pipeline.sh` launches one ephemeral cluster per run (1 primary, at most 2 core, on demand, auto-terminate); the next run starts only after the previous one ends. | Phase 14 ruling 6; [`scripts/README.md`](../scripts/README.md) |
+| YARN container size | EMR job 10 still ran out of memory at 50 and 100 percent (P14-D8, open); Spark completed on EMR only at 25 percent. | Phase 14 rulings 19, 21, 22 |
+| Neptune memory | Bulk loads run on `db.r6g.large`, then the instance returns to `db.t4g.medium`; a warm-up precedes the smoke test because cold-cache neighbor queries reached the 6 s query timeout. | Phase 14 rulings 42 to 47; [`docs/runbook.md`](runbook.md) |
+| ECS scale | Desired count 1 per service; maximums query-api 1, graph-evidence-api 2, worker 1. | `course.tfvars` `service_max_counts` |
+| Budget | Each cloud window had a time and cost cap with a stop rule (Phase 14 rulings 8 and 26; Phase 15 ruling 3); the runtime stage is destroyed between windows. | [`docs/agent-runs/phase-15/human-gate.md`](agent-runs/phase-15/human-gate.md) "Cost" |

+## 3. Public documentation consulted (accessed 2026-11-22)
+
+These pages were read through the aws-docs MCP server. The values are what the documentation states; they are not this account's quotas.
+
+| Page | What it states that matters here |
+|---|---|
+| [Quotas for Amazon Bedrock](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/quotas.html) | Model inference is controlled by per-model quotas; embedding models are throttled by requests per minute rather than tokens per minute. The quotas that apply to an account might be lower than the published defaults, and the values for an account are checked in Service Quotas. Increases are not granted automatically. |
+| [Amazon S3 Vectors limitations and restrictions](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/s3-vectors-limitations.html) | Dimension 1 to 4,096 per vector (ScholarChain uses 1024); up to 10 non-filterable metadata keys per index (ScholarChain declares 3: `vectorKey`, `textHash`, `textRef`); up to 50 metadata keys per vector; filterable metadata up to 2 KB per vector on `CLASSIC` indexes and 40 KB on `ENHANCED` indexes; up to 500 vectors per `PutVectors` call; up to 10,000 top-K results and up to 100 filter constraints per `QueryVectors` request on an `ENHANCED` index; up to 2 billion vectors per index. |
+| [emr-spark-8.0.0 release](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/emr/latest/ReleaseGuide/emr-spark800-release.html) | Initial release May 21, 2026; standard support until May 20, 2028. Applications listed: AmazonCloudWatchAgent, Delta, Hudi, Iceberg, JupyterEnterpriseGateway, Livy and Spark (4.0.2-amzn-0); no separate Hadoop application. Scala 2.13.16; AWS SDK for Java 2.41.32. |
+| [Choosing instance types for Amazon Neptune](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/neptune/latest/userguide/instance-types.html) | `t3` and `t4g` exist only in the medium size, are not intended for production, and are not recommended for testing query execution time. About two thirds of instance memory is buffer-pool cache; the page suggests a larger instance when the cache hit rate stays below 99.9 percent. Two query execution threads per vCPU. |
+| [Specifying the ARM architecture in an Amazon ECS task definition](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonECS/latest/developerguide/ecs-arm-specifying.html) | ARM is selected with `runtimePlatform.cpuArchitecture = ARM64` and `operatingSystemFamily = LINUX`. |
+| [Amazon EC2 instance type quotas](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/ec2/latest/instancetypes/ec2-instance-quotas.html) | On-Demand quotas are in vCPUs per Region; the documented default for Running On-Demand Standard instances is 5, adjustable, and EC2 raises quotas automatically with usage. This account's value at the time was 16 (section 1). |
